4	
5	# Code Review Workflow
6	
7	**Goal:** Review code changes adversarially using parallel review layers and structured triage.
8	
9	**Your Role:** You are an elite code reviewer. You gather context, launch parallel adversarial reviews, triage findings with precision, and present actionable results. No noise, no filler.
10	
11	
12	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
13	
14	This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:
15	
16	- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is self-contained and followed exactly
17	- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only load the current step file
18	- **Sequential Enforcement**: Complete steps in order, no skipping
19	- **State Tracking**: Persist progress via in-memory variables
20	- **Append-Only Building**: Build artifacts incrementally
21	
22	### Step Processing Rules
23	
24	1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Read the entire step file before acting
25	2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute sections in order
26	3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: Halt at checkpoints and wait for human
27	4. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
28	
29	### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
30	
31	- **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
32	- **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
33	- **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
34	- **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
35	- **ALWAYS** halt at checkpoints and wait for human input
36	
37	
38	## INITIALIZATION SEQUENCE
39	
40	### 1. Configuration Loading
41	
42	Load and read full config from `{main_config}` and resolve:
43	
44	- `project_name`, `planning_artifacts`, `implementation_artifacts`, `user_name`
45	- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `user_skill_level`
46	- `date` as system-generated current datetime
47	- `sprint_status` = `{implementation_artifacts}/sprint-status.yaml`
48	- `project_context` = `**/project-context.md` (load if exists)
49	- CLAUDE.md / memory files (load if exist)
50	
51	YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`.
52	
53	### 2. First Step Execution
54	
55	Read fully and follow: `./steps/step-01-gather-context.md` to begin the workflow.
56	
```

1	---
2	diff_output: '' # set at runtime
3	spec_file: '' # set at runtime (path or empty)
4	review_mode: '' # set at runtime: "full" or "no-spec"
5	story_key: '' # set at runtime when discovered from sprint status
6	---
7	
8	# Step 1: Gather Context
9	
10	## RULES
11	
12	- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
13	- The prompt that triggered this workflow IS the intent — not a hint.
14	- Do not modify any files. This step is read-only.
15	
16	## INSTRUCTIONS
17	
18	1. **Find the review target.** The conversation context before this skill was triggered IS your starting point — not a blank slate. Check in this order — stop as soon as the review target is identified:
19	
20	   **Tier 1 — Explicit argument.**
21	   Did the user pass a PR, commit SHA, branch, spec file, or diff source this message?
22	   - PR reference → resolve to branch/commit via `gh pr view`. If resolution fails, ask for a SHA or branch.
23	   - Commit or branch → use directly.
24	   - Spec file → set `{spec_file}` to the provided path. Check its frontmatter for `baseline_commit`. If found, use as diff baseline. If not found, continue the cascade (a spec alone does not identify a diff source).
25	   - Also scan the argument for diff-mode keywords that narrow the scope:
26	     - "staged" / "staged changes" → Staged changes only
27	     - "uncommitted" / "working tree" / "all changes" → Uncommitted changes (staged + unstaged)
28	     - "branch diff" / "vs main" / "against main" / "compared to <branch>" → Branch diff (extract base branch if mentioned)
29	     - "commit range" / "last N commits" / "<from-sha>..<to-sha>" → Specific commit range
30	     - "this diff" / "provided diff" / "paste" → User-provided diff (do not match bare "diff" — it appears in other modes)
31	   - When multiple keywords match, prefer the most specific (e.g., "branch diff" over bare "diff").
32	
33	   **Tier 2 — Recent conversation.**
34	   Do the last few messages reveal what the user wants to be reviewed? Look for spec paths, commit refs, branches, PRs, or descriptions of a change. Apply the same diff-mode keyword scan and routing as Tier 1.
35	
36	   **Tier 3 — Sprint tracking.**
37	   Look for a sprint status file (`*sprint-status*`) in `{implementation_artifacts}` or `{planning_artifacts}`. If found, scan for stories with status `review`:
38	   - **Exactly one `review` story:** Set `{story_key}` to the story's key (e.g., `1-2-user-auth`). Suggest it: "I found story <story-id> in `review` status. Would you like to review its changes? [Y] Yes / [N] No, let me choose". If confirmed, use the story context to determine the diff source (branch name derived from story slug, or uncommitted changes). If declined, clear `{story_key}` and fall through.
39	   - **Multiple `review` stories:** Present them as numbered options alongside a manual choice option. Wait for user selection. If a story is selected, set `{story_key}` and use its context to determine the diff source. If manual choice is selected, clear `{story_key}` and fall through.
40	   - **None:** Fall through.
41	
42	   **Tier 4 — Current git state.**
43	   If version control is unavailable, skip to Tier 5. Otherwise, check the current branch and HEAD. If the branch is not `main` (or the default branch), confirm: "I see HEAD is `<short-sha>` on `<branch>` — do you want to review this branch's changes?" If confirmed, treat as a branch diff against `main`. If declined, fall through.
44	
45	   **Tier 5 — Ask.**
46	   Fall through to instruction 2.
47	
48	   Never ask extra questions beyond what the cascade prescribes. If a tier above already identified the target, skip the remaining tiers and proceed to instruction 3 (construct diff).
49	
50	2. HALT. Ask the user: **What do you want to review?** Present these options:
51	   - **Uncommitted changes** (staged + unstaged)
52	   - **Staged changes only**
53	   - **Branch diff** vs a base branch (ask which base branch)
54	   - **Specific commit range** (ask for the range)
55	   - **Provided diff or file list** (user pastes or provides a path)
56	
57	3. Construct `{diff_output}` from the chosen source.
58	   - For **staged changes only**: run `git diff --cached`.
59	   - For **uncommitted changes** (staged + unstaged): run `git diff HEAD`.
60	   - For **branch diff**: verify the base branch exists before running `git diff`. If it does not exist, HALT and ask the user for a valid branch.
61	   - For **commit range**: verify the range resolves. If it does not, HALT and ask the user for a valid range.
62	   - For **provided diff**: validate the content is non-empty and parseable as a unified diff. If it is not parseable, HALT and ask the user to provide a valid diff.
63	   - For **file list**: validate each path exists in the working tree. Construct `{diff_output}` by running `git diff HEAD -- <path1> <path2> ...`. If any paths are untracked (new files not yet staged), use `git diff --no-index /dev/null <path>` to include them. If the diff is empty (files have no uncommitted changes and are not untracked), ask the user whether to review the full file contents or to specify a different baseline.
64	   - After constructing `{diff_output}`, verify it is non-empty regardless of source type. If empty, HALT and tell the user there is nothing to review.
65	
66	4. **Set the spec context.**
67	   - If `{spec_file}` is already set (from Tier 1 or Tier 2): verify the file exists and is readable, then set `{review_mode}` = `"full"`.
68	   - Otherwise, ask the user: **Is there a spec or story file that provides context for these changes?**
69	     - If yes: set `{spec_file}` to the path provided, verify the file exists and is readable, then set `{review_mode}` = `"full"`.
70	     - If no: set `{review_mode}` = `"no-spec"`.
71	
72	5. If `{review_mode}` = `"full"` and the file at `{spec_file}` has a `context` field in its frontmatter listing additional docs, load each referenced document. Warn the user about any docs that cannot be found.
73	
74	6. Sanity check: if `{diff_output}` exceeds approximately 3000 lines, warn the user and offer to chunk the review by file group.
75	   - If the user opts to chunk: agree on the first group, narrow `{diff_output}` accordingly, and list the remaining groups for the user to note for follow-up runs.
76	   - If the user declines: proceed as-is with the full diff.
77	
78	### CHECKPOINT
79	
80	Present a summary before proceeding: diff stats (files changed, lines added/removed), `{review_mode}`, and loaded spec/context docs (if any). HALT and wait for user confirmation to proceed.
81	
82	
83	## NEXT
84	
85	Read fully and follow `./step-02-review.md`
86	
```

1	# Story 5.1: Pre-v1.0.0 readiness audit
2	
3	Status: review
4	
5	<!-- Note: Validation is optional. Run validate-create-story for quality check before dev-story. -->
6	
7	## Story
8	
9	As the maintainer,
10	I want a final gate audit that records the live state of every must-have launch-checklist item AND closes the known pre-flight gaps inherited from Epics 1–4 retros (H1–H10 + R1–R8 dispositions, plus the `### Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1` hand-bump procedure),
11	so that when Story 5.2 dispatches `release.yaml` with `version_bump: rc`, the RC cycle tests release mechanics against a truly launch-ready artifact — not against still-in-progress docs, an un-anchored sed clobber risk, a silent-exit-0 install.js, or a Starlight sidebar that doesn't surface the contract.
12	
13	## Acceptance Criteria
14	
15	1. **Scope envelope — Story 5.1 touches EXACTLY seven files** (one new, six modified). Files: (a) `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` (NEW — the audit artifact per epic AC "the current commit SHA on `main` SHALL be recorded in the audit artifact"); (b) `docs/STABILITY.md` (MODIFIED — DRAFT→STABLE banner flip, H6); (c) `CHANGELOG.md` (MODIFIED — insert hand-curated `## [1.0.0] - TBD` entry from `v1-0-0-changelog-draft.md` content; H5; also add `(pre-v0.3.0 tag, never published)` parenthetical to the dangling `Revert "0.2.0"` at `CHANGELOG.md:259` per deferred-work 2-2); (d) `docs/RELEASING.md` (MODIFIED — author new `### Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1` H3 section at end of Rollback Playbook cluster, H8; add R4/R5/R6 one-line caveats to Scenarios A/C/C); (e) `tools/cli/commands/install.js` (MODIFIED — add explicit `else { process.exit(1); }` for falsy-`result.success` path, H1); (f) `.github/workflows/release.yaml` (MODIFIED — replace unanchored `sed` on `marketplace.json` with `jq`-based in-place update scoped to `plugins[0]`, H3); (g) `website/astro.config.mjs` (MODIFIED — register `stability` and `releasing` slugs under the Reference sidebar bucket, H4). **NO changes to:** `package.json` (version / main / bin / scripts — all release-workflow territory for Story 5.2+); `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (version sync is release-workflow territory); `README.md` (Story 4.1 already aligned); `CONTRIBUTING.md` (Story 4.2 already aligned); `.github/workflows/publish.yaml` / `manual-release.yaml` (Story 6.1 deletion territory); `tools/cli/skf-cli.js` (no flags to add per STABILITY.md § "Per-subcommand flags: none are currently part of the v1.0.0 surface"); any `src/**` / `test/**` / `_bmad-output/**` (other than this story file). **Verification:** `git status --short` after commit SHALL show exactly 7 entries: `A docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` + `M docs/STABILITY.md` + `M CHANGELOG.md` + `M docs/RELEASING.md` + `M tools/cli/commands/install.js` + `M .github/workflows/release.yaml` + `M website/astro.config.mjs`.
16	
17	2. **Baseline snapshot — `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` records the current commit SHA on `main`** (epic AC verbatim: "the current commit SHA on `main` SHALL be recorded in the audit artifact as the baseline for the RC cut"). Expected baseline SHA at create-story time: `5e407a2` (PR #195 merge closing Epic 4). Re-verify at Task 1 recon via `git log main --oneline -1 --format=%H` — if `main` has advanced, record the ACTUAL `main` HEAD SHA; do not paste the stale `5e407a2`. 38	10. **H1 — `tools/cli/commands/install.js` silent-exit-0 on `{success: false}` resolved.** Current shape at `tools/cli/commands/install.js:23-26`: `if (result && result.success) { ui.displaySuccess(...); process.exit(0); }` — falsy `result.success` falls through to an implicit function return, which exits the process with code 0 (contradicts `docs/STABILITY.md § User-Facing Command Output Formats`: "`skf install` SHALL exit `0` on successful install and on user-cancel ... `1` on unhandled failure"). **Fix:** add an explicit `else { process.exit(1); }` branch so a falsy `result.success` exits 1. Final shape (verbatim target):
39	    ```javascript
40	          if (result && result.success) {
41	            ui.displaySuccess(config.skfFolder, config.ides, config._action);
42	            process.exit(0);
43	          } else {
44	            console.error(chalk.red('\nInstallation failed.'));
45	            process.exit(1);
46	          }
47	    ```
48	    **Why `else` + `process.exit(1)` specifically (not a bare `process.exit(1)` after the `if`):** a bare `process.exit(1)` after the `if`-`return` form would still work, but the `else`-form reads symmetrically with the existing `catch` block at lines 27–31 and keeps the success + failure branches visually paired. **Defensive message routing:** the `chalk.red('\nInstallation failed.')` message goes to `stderr` (via `console.error`) following the Story 4.2 patch `41ccf35` precedent that diagnostic notices belong on stderr (Epic 4 retro Commitment 4 generalization: diagnostic output → stderr). Verification at Task 4: inject a test where `installer.install()` returns `{success: false}` (or simply returns `undefined`), run via a mock harness or ad-hoc `node -e`, confirm `$? = 1`. Do NOT change the existing `try`/`catch`, `process.exit(0)` on `config.cancelled`, `ui.promptInstall`, or `Installer` logic — the surgical diff is the two lines inside the `else`. **Files touched:** `tools/cli/commands/install.js` only; NOT `tools/cli/commands/{update,status,uninstall}.js` (recon confirms those three already exit 1 on failure paths; only `install.js` has this defect).
49	
50	11. **H3 — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:115` `marketplace.json` sed clobber hardened via `jq`.** Current shape at `release.yaml:113-115`:
51	    ```yaml
52	          - name: Update marketplace.json version
53	            run: |
54	              sed -i 's/"version": "[^"]*"/"version": "${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}"/' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json
55	    ```
56	    The `sed` pattern is unanchored on both sides — if `marketplace.json` ever grows a second `"version":` key (e.g., schema version), `sed` replaces the first match which may not be the plugin version. Current state (verified at create-story time): `jq '[paths(scalars) | select(.[-1] == "version")] | length' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json` returns `1` (single version field at `.plugins[0].version`). **Fix:** replace the `sed` block with `jq` targeted at `.plugins[0].version` using a temp file + atomic rename (jq has no in-place edit). Final shape (verbatim target):
57	    ```yaml
58	          - name: Update marketplace.json version
59	            run: |
60	              # Targeted JSON edit scoped to .plugins[0].version — resistant to future
61	              # schema additions of other `version` keys. Uses a temp file + atomic
62	              # rename so a partial-write mid-step cannot leave the file in a broken
63	              # shape (jq has no in-place edit). The VERSION variable is interpolated
64	              # via jq --arg to avoid shell-quote hazards.
65	              VERSION='${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}'
66	              jq --arg v "$VERSION" '.plugins[0].version = $v' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json > .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp
67	              mv .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp .claude-plugin/marketplace.json
68	              # Sanity check: the written version matches what we set.
69	              WROTE=$(jq -r '.plugins[0].version' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json)
70	              if [ "$WROTE" != "$VERSION" ]; then
71	                echo "::error::marketplace.json version write failed: expected $VERSION, got $WROTE"; exit 1
72	              fi
73	    ```
74	    **Why `jq` and not anchored `sed`:** anchored `sed` (e.g., `sed -E '/"plugins": /,/^  \]/{ s/"version": "[^"]*"/"version": "..."/ }'`) is fragile to formatting changes (indentation, line breaks, trailing commas); `jq` parses the JSON structure and cannot clobber adjacent fields. GitHub's `ubuntu-latest` runner ships `jq` by default — no `apt install` required. Pre-existing repo convention: `.github/workflows/*.yaml` already uses `jq` in multiple places (e.g., `quality.yaml` test runs). Verify at Task 5 via a local dry-run against the current `marketplace.json`: `VERSION=9.9.9 jq --arg v "$VERSION" '.plugins[0].version = $v' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json > /tmp/out.json && diff <(jq . .claude-plugin/marketplace.json) <(jq . /tmp/out.json) | head -5` — the diff SHALL show only the `"version":` line flipping from `"0.10.0"` to `"9.9.9"`. Do NOT commit the dry-run scratch files. NO changes to `release.yaml` lines outside the one `Update marketplace.json version` step.

321	## Dev Notes
322	
323	### What this story actually is
324	
325	A **seven-file pre-flight closure pass** that records the state of every Epic 1–4 gate AND closes the known pre-flight gaps inherited from Epic 4 retro (H1 / H3 / H4 / H5 / H6 / H8) so that Story 5.2's `version_bump: rc` dispatch operates against a truly launch-ready artifact. The dev agent's temptation here is to treat this as a "just author the audit artifact" story and skip the gap-fix edits — **resist that**. Epic 4 retro Commitment 2 is load-bearing: "Story 5.1 `create-story` MUST import Epic 5 backlog items H1–H10 into Dev Notes as the audit scope." The audit IS the gap closure; they are inseparable in this one story.
326	
327	**What this story is NOT:**
328	
329	- NOT a `package.json` version bump (Story 5.2 hand-bump territory; AC 1 envelope explicit).
330	- NOT an `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` version sync (Story 5.2 release-workflow territory; AC 1 envelope explicit).
331	- NOT a release cut — no `npm publish`, no git tag, no GitHub Release creation.
332	- NOT a `tools/cli/skf-cli.js` refactor to add per-subcommand flags (per `docs/STABILITY.md:20` the v1.0.0 surface has NO per-subcommand flags; adding any would expand the public API pre-v1.0.0).
333	- NOT a `README.md` edit (Story 4.1 already aligned the Release Process link; AC 1 envelope explicit).
334	- NOT a `CONTRIBUTING.md` edit (Story 4.2 already authored the `## Releasing` section; AC 1 envelope explicit).
335	- NOT a deletion of `publish.yaml` / `manual-release.yaml` (Story 6.1 territory; they remain as inert backups until post-v1.0.0).
336	- NOT a fix to R1 / R2 / R3 / R7 / R8 Rollback Playbook deferrals (each requires investigation/drill unsuitable for this story; DEFERRED-POST-V1).
337	- NOT a revert of the Story 4.2 `[DEPRECATED]`-on-stderr divergence (Epic 4 retro Commitment 4 recommendation: keep stderr routing; AC 19 records the decision).
338	- NOT a `prevent_self_review: true` flip on the `release` environment (H9 standing flag; couples with second-maintainer onboarding).
339	- NOT a cross-file anchor validator addition (H10 DEFERRED-POST-V1; pre-existing tooling gap).

400	### Load-bearing decisions already made at create-story (no Dev-Agent re-derivation needed)
401	
402	**(A) Seven-file envelope, not two.** Unlike Stories 4.1 and 4.2 (which held to two-file envelopes), Story 5.1 is the "consolidation gate" and must close H1 / H3 / H4 / H5 / H6 / H8 in one coherent PR — the audit artifact would be misleading if it recorded "gap found and also fixed in a separate PR" without the fixes actually landing. Splitting into multiple stories (5.1a audit, 5.1b fix install.js, 5.1c sidebar, …) bloats the story count and breaks the audit-with-close-out narrative. Epic 4 retro Commitment 2 anticipated this ("Story 5.1 owns H1–H10"). The envelope is bigger because the story is fundamentally cross-cutting.
403	
404	**(B) CHANGELOG placement: `## [1.0.0] - TBD` between `[Unreleased]` and `[0.10.0]`, not at the literal top.** The epic AC says "added at the top" which could be read as "above [Unreleased]", but Keep-a-Changelog convention (which STABILITY.md already commits to) places `[Unreleased]` at the very top as a stable anchor and semver versions below in reverse chronological. Placing `[1.0.0]` above `[Unreleased]` would make `[Unreleased]` dead content. Placing below `[Unreleased]` and above `[0.10.0]` keeps the convention AND puts `[1.0.0]` at the top of the versioned history. The `- TBD` placeholder signals that the date is staged for Story 5.3's reconciliation. See AC 5 / AC 13 for the mechanics; see AC 15's Outcome bullet for the post-RC-cut shape.
405	
406	**(C) `### Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1` at the END of the Rollback Playbook cluster, not in its own H2.** Strictly, "cutting an RC" is not "rollback"; it's the antipode. But: (i) the Rollback Playbook cluster is operationally grouped (`gh api` / `npm view` / CLI procedures under incident pressure), and the RC hand-bump procedure is operationally the same shape (Trigger / CLI / Outcome / Constraints / Verification); (ii) creating a new H2 `## Pre-Launch Procedures` for just one subsection is over-structured; (iii) the section's tight coupling with Scenarios D/E (tag-orphan / state-drift) means proximity helps the reader — if the RC cut fails, Scenarios D/E are the immediate-next-doc-scroll. Section title says "Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1" (not "Pre-Launch: …") so the H3-under-Rollback-Playbook visual hierarchy is the main surprise; the title itself is unambiguous.
407	
408	**(D) `jq` over `anchored sed` for H3 marketplace.json fix.** The fragility of `sed`-based JSON editing comes from the pattern's sensitivity to formatting (indentation, key order, trailing commas). `jq` parses structure and is immune to those. Runner availability + repo convention both favor `jq`. The cost is a temp file + atomic rename (jq has no in-place edit) — 2 extra lines, negligible. Anchored `sed` would work today but we want a fix that survives future schema changes; the whole point of H3 is future-proofing the sync step for exactly the "second version field added later" scenario.
409	
410	**(E) `[DEPRECATED]`-on-stderr divergence: KEEP.** Epic 4 retro Commitment 4 recommendation verbatim: "keep the stderr routing — it is the convention for diagnostic notices and Story 6.2's delete-the-scripts is unaffected by the routing choice." AC 19 records this decision in the audit artifact without re-editing `package.json`.
411	
412	**(F) Audit artifact location: `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md`.** Epic AC offers a choice ("e.g., `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` or issue comment"). File beats issue comment because: (i) file is audit-trail in-repo alongside `RELEASING.md` and `STABILITY.md`; (ii) Story 5.2 / 5.3 / 5.4 can append completion markers to the same file; (iii) the Sign-off section is a durable artifact the maintainer edits once at dispatch time. File location `docs/` (alongside STABILITY / RELEASING) matches the contract-document convention.
413	
414	**(G) Branch strategy: fresh branch from `main`, not rebase `feat/release-workflow`.** The legacy branch is preserved only for the orphaned `v0.10.1-alpha.0` commit (Epic 3 retro standing flag #15). Rebasing it would risk dragging old Epic 3 reflogs into the Story 5.1 PR. A fresh `feat/v1-readiness-audit` branch from `main` (post-`5e407a2`) is cleaner.
415

Now let me look at the beginning of CHANGELOG changes again to confirm the order:

Line 32: `## [Unreleased]`
Line 34 (added): `## [1.0.0] - TBD`
Line 95: `## [0.10.0]...(2026-04-06)`

So the order is `[Unreleased]` → `[1.0.0] - TBD` → `[0.10.0]`. The `[1.0.0]` is positioned between Unreleased and 0.10.0, but [Unreleased] is now empty (no content between Unreleased header and [1.0.0] header). Let me verify.

Looking at lines 30-34:
```
 ## [Unreleased]
 
+## [1.0.0] - TBD
+
+<!--
```

`[Unreleased]` is immediately followed by `[1.0.0] - TBD` with nothing in between. This is fine per Keep-a-Changelog.

Now let me draft the findings.

---

## Findings

### 1. **`CHANGELOG.md` reshuffles every pre-existing bullet from `* ` to `- ` (wholesale style flip)**
- **File:line**: `CHANGELOG.md:99-497` (across all historical release sections)
- **Evidence**: Every historical `* add …`, `* fix …` line is removed and re-added as `- add …`, `- fix …`, e.g.
  ```
  -* add plugin reload hint to export-skill summary ([4a55109]…)
  +- add plugin reload hint to export-skill summary ([4a55109]…)
  ```
- **Severity**: MEDIUM
- **Why it matters**: This is a blast-radius change for a "readiness audit" PR — hundreds of lines of historical changelog are rewritten to switch bullet markers. Future auto-generated changelog bumps (released by `release.yaml` via `npm version` / conventional-changelog tooling) will still emit `* ` bullets, so the very next automated run will produce a mixed-style file with a noisy diff, and the "one-time cleanup" will have to be redone on every release. If the project uses `conventional-changelog-cli`, the style flip fights the tool.

### 2. **`Revert "0.2.0"` line's hyperlink target changed as a side-effect of the annotation**
- **File:line**: `CHANGELOG.md:496-497`
- **Evidence**:
  ```
  -* Revert "0.2.0" ([ed2df11]…)
  +- Revert "0.2.0" (pre-v0.3.0 tag, never published) ([ed2df11]…)
  ```
- **Severity**: LOW
- **Why it matters**: The annotation itself is fine, but combining it with the bullet-style flip and the parenthetical means this is the only historical line semantically edited — easy to miss in review that the *content* (not just the marker) was touched. The `(pre-v0.3.0 tag, never published)` parenthetical is inserted between the title and the link, so the rendered output reads "Revert "0.2.0" (pre-v0.3.0 tag, never published) ([ed2df11])" — the parenthetical is not in italics or any delimiter, and could be mistaken for part of the commit title.

### 3. **Blank line between trigger bullet and blockquote breaks the list item in Scenario A**
- **File:line**: `docs/RELEASING.md:505-508`
- **Evidence**:
  ```
  - **Trigger.** Maintainer notices a defect within minutes of publish…
  +
  +  > **Note on cache lag.** `npm view … dist-tags` reads…
  +
  - **CLI.**
  ```
- **Severity**: LOW
- **Why it matters**: The `>` blockquote sits between two `-` list items with blank lines on either side. Most CommonMark renderers will terminate the first list item at the blank line and render the blockquote as a top-level block, then start a fresh list with `- **CLI.**`. If the existing rendering depends on "Scenario A has five bullets in one list", the new blockquote visually splits that list into two sublists. Verify under Starlight.

### 4. **Blank line between `}` and hyphen in the italicization re-flow**
- **File:line**: `docs/RELEASING.md:564-566`
- **Evidence**:
  ```
  @@ -478,7 +484,6 @@ …
     Read the next two paragraphs **before** dispatching:
  -
     - **Auth vs. provenance…**
  ```
- **Severity**: LOW
- **Why it matters**: A blank line between the intro sentence "Read the next two paragraphs **before** dispatching:" and the nested `- **Auth vs. provenance…**` bullet was REMOVED. In CommonMark, removing the blank line means the bullet may be treated as a continuation paragraph of the preceding line rather than as a new sublist item — the rendering may coalesce. This diff reads as "cosmetic tightening" but the markdown semantics changed.

### 5. **`release.yaml` quoting — single-quoted shell string with GitHub expression works by luck, fails on an apostrophe**
- **File:line**: `.github/workflows/release.yaml:15`
- **Evidence**:
  ```
  VERSION='${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}'
  ```
- **Severity**: LOW
- **Why it matters**: GitHub Actions expands `${{ … }}` textually into the script BEFORE `bash -e` parses it. If `steps.version.outputs.new_version` ever contained a single quote (e.g. from a malformed prerelease-id that the earlier step failed to sanitize), the outer single-quote string would terminate prematurely and the remainder would be parsed as shell. Normal SemVer strings are safe, so this is low, but the commit comment *explicitly* promises "the VERSION variable is interpolated via jq `--arg` to avoid shell-quote hazards" — which is only half true: `jq --arg` protects the jq filter, not the shell line above it. Consider using env injection (`env: VERSION: ${{ … }}`) instead of single-quote interpolation.

### 6. **`release.yaml` jq step exits non-zero on mv race, but does not clean up `.tmp` on jq failure**
- **File:line**: `.github/workflows/release.yaml:16-17`
- **Evidence**:
  ```
  jq --arg v "$VERSION" '.plugins[0].version = $v' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json > .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp
  mv .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp .claude-plugin/marketplace.json
  ```
- **Severity**: LOW
- **Why it matters**: If `jq` fails mid-write, the shell redirection will leave a truncated `.tmp` file on disk. `set -e` (default in GitHub Actions with `bash`) will abort before the `mv`, so `marketplace.json` is preserved — good. But the stray `.tmp` file is now committed state if a subsequent step runs `git add -A`. The comment claims "a partial-write mid-step cannot leave the file in a broken shape" which is true for the target file, but the comment does not acknowledge the `.tmp` residue risk.

### 7. **`release.yaml` sanity check uses exit 1 instead of failing via `set -e`**
- **File:line**: `.github/workflows/release.yaml:18-22`
- **Evidence**:
  ```
  WROTE=$(jq -r '.plugins[0].version' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json)
  if [ "$WROTE" != "$VERSION" ]; then
    echo "::error::marketplace.json version write failed: expected $VERSION, got $WROTE"; exit 1
  fi
  ```
- **Severity**: NIT
- **Why it matters**: If `jq -r` fails (malformed JSON after the write), the `$(…)` captures an empty string and set -e is suppressed inside command-substitution-assigned-to-variable. The comparison then compares `""` against `$VERSION`, so it still exits 1, but the error message is misleading ("expected X, got " with empty `got`). Consider `WROTE=$(jq -r … ) || { echo "::error::jq re-read failed"; exit 1; }`.

## Edge Case Hunter Report — Story 5.1

### Unhandled edge cases

- **`jq` reformats `marketplace.json` keywords array into multi-line shape**
  - **File:line**: `.github/workflows/release.yaml:121` (diff line 16)
  - **Path walked**: Dry-ran `jq --arg v "1.0.0-rc.1" '.plugins[0].version = $v' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json` on HEAD. Input has `keywords` as a compact single-line array; jq emits it across 18 lines (one key per line).
  - **Gap**: Every release commit will carry a spurious 18-line diff on `keywords` in addition to the 1-line `version` change. The first RC cut commit will show a ~20-line marketplace.json diff that reviewers must visually verify is not a regression (keywords unchanged, just reflowed). Prettier does NOT reject either shape (printWidth 140 tolerates both), so CI passes — but the repo permanently flips to multi-line keywords format at first release and any future hand-edits restoring compact form will get reflowed again at next release. Minor confusion risk; more importantly, the `docs/RELEASING.md § Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1` "Expected outcome" section claims `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json's .plugins[0].version reflects 1.0.0-rc.1` but does NOT warn about the keywords reflow.
  - **Severity**: MEDIUM
  - **How to verify**: `jq --arg v "1.0.0-rc.1" '.plugins[0].version = $v' /home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json | diff - /home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json | wc -l` → reports 19 diff lines not 3.

- **`install.js` `else` branch is dead code — `installer.install()` never returns `{success: false}`**
  - **File:line**: `tools/cli/commands/install.js:26-29` (diff lines 885-888)
  - **Path walked**: Enumerated every `return` in `tools/cli/lib/installer.js`: the function returns `{ success: true, skfDir, projectDir }` on line 147 (only terminal return) and `throw`s on every error path (lines 66, 76, 89, 99, 110, 129, 144). There is NO code path that returns `null`, `undefined`, a `{success: false}` object, or anything else non-truthy.
  - **Gap**: The new `else { console.error('Installation failed.'); process.exit(1); }` block is unreachable with the current installer contract. All real failures already go through the existing `catch` branch (lines 30-34 post-patch) which prints a more informative message (`'Installation failed:'` + `error.message` + stack trace). The fix defends against a hypothetical future `{success: false}` return contract that does not exist. Worse, the new branch prints a generic `'\nInstallation failed.'` with NO context — if the contract ever IS added, the diagnostic is strictly worse than the `catch` branch because it loses the error reason.
  - **Severity**: LOW (defensive but inert; no actual bug introduced)
  - **How to verify**: `grep -n 'return' /home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/tools/cli/lib/installer.js` — only one `return {...}` (line 147) and it always has `success: true`.

- **Sibling `tools/cli/commands/update.js` has the EXACT same silent-exit-0 pattern the H1 fix was meant to address — left untouched**
  - **File:line**: `tools/cli/commands/update.js:43-57` (not in diff)
  - **Path walked**: H1 backlog title is "silent-exit-0 on `{success: false}`" — fixed in `install.js`. But `update.js` does `if (result && result.success) { ui.displaySuccess(...) }` then ALWAYS calls `process.exit(0)` unconditionally on line 57, regardless of whether success branch ran. If `installer.install()` ever returns falsy, `update` command exits 0 silently (no success message, no error).
  - **Gap**: The fix for H1 is incomplete — the same anti-pattern lives in `update.js` and was not addressed. The audit file's H1 entry names only `install.js:23-26`, so the gap was not surfaced in the scope discussion either.

- **`docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` becomes a PUBLIC page on the Starlight site but has no sidebar entry**

Astro's default slug generator is `github-slugger`, which yields `v100-launch-audit` (dots stripped).

4	# Step 3: Triage
5	
6	## RULES
7	
8	- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
9	- Be precise. When uncertain between categories, prefer the more conservative classification.
10	
11	## INSTRUCTIONS
12	
13	1. **Normalize** findings into a common format. Expected input formats:
14	   - Adversarial (Blind Hunter): markdown list of descriptions
15	   - Edge Case Hunter: JSON array with `location`, `trigger_condition`, `guard_snippet`, `potential_consequence` fields
16	   - Acceptance Auditor: markdown list with title, AC/constraint reference, and evidence
17	
18	   If a layer's output does not match its expected format, attempt best-effort parsing. Note any parsing issues for the user.
19	
20	   Convert all to a unified list where each finding has:
21	   - `id` -- sequential integer
22	   - `source` -- `blind`, `edge`, `auditor`, or merged sources (e.g., `blind+edge`)
23	   - `title` -- one-line summary
24	   - `detail` -- full description
25	   - `location` -- file and line reference (if available)
26	
27	2. **Deduplicate.** If two or more findings describe the same issue, merge them into one:
28	   - Use the most specific finding as the base (prefer edge-case JSON with location over adversarial prose).
29	   - Append any unique detail, reasoning, or location references from the other finding(s) into the surviving `detail` field.
30	   - Set `source` to the merged sources (e.g., `blind+edge`).
31	
32	3. **Classify** each finding into exactly one bucket:
33	   - **decision_needed** -- There is an ambiguous choice that requires human input. The code cannot be correctly patched without knowing the user's intent. Only possible if `{review_mode}` = `"full"`.
34	   - **patch** -- Code issue that is fixable without human input. The correct fix is unambiguous.
35	   - **defer** -- Pre-existing issue not caused by the current change. Real but not actionable now.
36	   - **dismiss** -- Noise, false positive, or handled elsewhere.
37	
38	   If `{review_mode}` = `"no-spec"` and a finding would otherwise be `decision_needed`, reclassify it as `patch` (if the fix is unambiguous) or `defer` (if not).
39	
40	4. **Drop** all `dismiss` findings. Record the dismiss count for the summary.
41	
42	5. If `{failed_layers}` is non-empty, report which layers failed before announcing results. If zero findings remain after dropping dismissed AND `{failed_layers}` is non-empty, warn the user that the review may be incomplete rather than announcing a clean review.
43	
44	6. If zero findings remain after triage (all rejected or none raised): state "✅ Clean review — all layers passed." (Step 3 already warned if any review layers failed via `{failed_layers}`.)
45	
46	
47	## NEXT
48	
49	Read fully and follow `./step-04-present.md`
50

23	1. **`decision-needed`** findings (unchecked):

26	2. **`patch`** findings (unchecked):

Walk through each finding (or batch related ones) and get the user's call. Once resolved, each becomes a `patch`, `defer`, or is dismissed.

66	> 0. **Batch-apply all** — automatically fix every non-controversial patch (recommended when there are many)
67	> 1. **Fix them automatically** — I will apply fixes now
68	> 2. **Walk through each** — let me show details before deciding

70	**HALT** — I am waiting for your numbered choice. Reply with only the number (or "0" for batch). Do not proceed until you select an option.

72	- **Option 0** (only when >3 findings): Apply all non-controversial patches without per-finding confirmation. Skip any finding that requires judgment. Present a summary of changes made and any skipped findings.
73	- **Option 1**: Apply each fix. After all patches are applied, present a summary of changes made. If `{spec_file}` is set, check off the items in the story file.
74	- **Option 2** (only when `{spec_file}` is set): Done — findings are already written to the story.
75	- **Walk through each**: Present each finding with full detail, diff context, and suggested fix. After walkthrough, re-offer the applicable options above.
76

- **[HIGH] CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` anchor lifecycle is structurally broken by `conventional-changelog-cli -s` append mode** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:87-89` (`Update CHANGELOG.md` step) against `CHANGELOG.md`. Story 3.2's alpha cut (the designated live validator for this deferred-work from Story 3.1 review) produced concrete evidence: the preset prepends the new release entry at byte 0 of the infile, pushing the `# Changelog` title, Keep-a-Changelog preamble, and `## [Unreleased]` anchor DOWN below the new entry. Post-cut shape of `git show v0.10.1-alpha.0:CHANGELOG.md`: `line 1: ## [0.10.1-alpha.0]...`, `line 118: # Changelog`, `line 120: All notable changes...`, `line 122: ## [Unreleased]`, `line 124: ## [0.10.0]...`. Every subsequent release compounds the disorder. **Decision (Story 3.2 close-out, user-approved):** defer the fix to Story 3.3's merge PR (not Story 3.2 scope expansion). Story 3.3 should add a post-generation step to `release.yaml` that restores the preamble block to the top of the file after the preset runs — either via a small Python helper, a sed/awk pipeline, or switching to a generation approach that honors the `[Unreleased]` anchor as the insertion point. If Story 3.3 does NOT bundle this, the FIRST main-branch release cut will ship with the malformed shape and every release thereafter will compound it. Story 5.1 (pre-v1.0.0 audit) is the later backstop if Story 3.3 punts. Evidence: Story 3.2 Dev Agent Record § Debug Log References § AC #10 lifecycle Q1/Q2/Q3 answers.
- **[MED] Dev agent wrote `AC #N` in commit bodies; preset parsed `#N` as GH issue refs** — `0.10.1-alpha.0` CHANGELOG entry (orphaned commit `2a57dcbd`; also preserved in the published tarball's CHANGELOG). Four defect-fix commits (`f449c67`, `cbc7851`, `de2334b`, `cae5861`) had bodies referencing `AC #11` / `AC #12` meaning Acceptance Criteria — the conventionalcommits preset interpreted these as GitHub issue references and emitted `closes [#11]` / `closes [#12]` links in the CHANGELOG. The `Assert no bogus issue refs` guard correctly did NOT fire (refs are numeric; the guard targets the Story 2.2 class-of-bug of non-numeric refs). Cosmetic defect in a throwaway alpha entry. **Process fix for all future dev agents:** never write `AC #N` in commit bodies when the repo uses conventional-changelog tooling — write `AC N`, `AC-N`, or `acceptance criterion N`. Remediation options: (a) hand-correct the 0.10.1-alpha.0 CHANGELOG entry in Story 5.1's audit pass, (b) accept as historical artifact since AC #9 preserves the alpha as evidence only (no consumer will install `@alpha` intentionally pre-v1.0.0). Recommendation: (b) — low-value edit with npm-side mutability constraints (published tarball immutable).
- **[LOW] `f449c67` (first failed `--force` fix attempt) is dead-code in the feat/release-workflow chain** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml`. The `--force` approach was fully superseded by `cbc7851`'s side-prefix install; `f449c67`'s step body was overwritten one commit later. Preserving per Story 3.2 AC #11 "no amend / no force-push" rule — the commit chain is the audit trail of the debugging path. Story 3.3's merge PR will carry it as noise in the commit-list diff view. **Action:** none; Story 3.3 reviewer should be aware the 4 `fix(release):` commits include one superseded intermediate. Acceptable cost for audit integrity.
- **[LOW] GitHub-hosted runner's bundled npm (Node 22.22.2 → npm 10.9.7) falls below the trusted-publisher floor of 11.5.1** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml` `Ensure npm CLI supports trusted publishing` step. Side-prefix install resolves it but adds ~15s to every release run. Future optimization: when a Node 22.x release ships bundled npm ≥11.5.1 by default, conditionally skip the bootstrap step (`if: npm_version < 11.5.1`). Not blocking. Tracked as a Story 5.1 NFR1 (15-min CI target) cycle-time data point.
- **[INFO] Node.js 20 deprecation warnings on every run for `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4`, `astral-sh/setup-uv@v6`, `softprops/action-gh-release@v2`** — all repo workflows. GitHub forcing Node 24 by 2026-06-02; Node 20 removed 2026-09-16. Repo-wide upgrade scope, not Story 3.2. Two paths: rely on action maintainers to publish Node-24-compatible versions before the deadline (most will), or set `FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24=true` at workflow level now. Defer to a post-v1.0.0 maintenance story.
- **[INFO] `astral-sh/setup-uv@v6` cache-miss warning on every release run** — `release.yaml:54-57`. "No file matched to ... requirements.txt ... pyproject.toml ... uv.lock". Repo has Python test files (pytest via uv) but no Python lockfile, so uv's cache never fills. `npm test` (including Python tests) still passes; cache never invalidates. Low-priority maintenance cleanup — add a `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` in the Python test dir, or silence the warning via `setup-uv`'s cache-dependency-glob input. Defer.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-2-validate-release-yaml-via-alpha-test-cut-from-feature-branch (2026-04-21)

- **Substring-based `alpha|beta|rc` tag classification, now doubled by dry-run mirror** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:117-123` (dry-run) + `:184-190` (real publish). The Story 3.2 fix `de2334b` added the dry-run case-chain byte-parallel to Story 3.1's publish chain — correct for AC #5 consistency, but the substring pattern (`*"alpha"*`, `*"beta"*`, `*"rc"*`) can misclassify unusual semver strings. Concrete example from the spec (line 739): a future `1.0.0-march.1` would match `*"rc"*` in the case-chain. Proper fix is semver-aware parsing or anchoring to the prerelease identifier segment. Coordinated change to both chains. Track with Story 3.3 or split story.
- **Empty `$VERSION` silently routes to `@latest`** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:117-123` (dry-run) + `:184-190` (real publish). Neither case-chain guards `[[ -z "$VERSION" ]]`. If `steps.version.outputs.new_version` is ever empty (bump step failed without aborting, corrupted `package.json` mid-job, etc.), `TAG=latest` and the package publishes to `@latest`. Coordinated `[[ -z "$VERSION" ]] && exit 1` guard belongs with the substring-collision fix above.
- **`npm ci` regenerates `.husky/_/` wrappers on CI; `HUSKY: "0"` is the sole safety net** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:75` (`Install dependencies` runs `npm ci` which fires the `"prepare": "husky || exit 0"` lifecycle) + `:125` (`Commit version bump` which would trip `.husky/commit-msg` without `HUSKY=0`). Defense-in-depth: add `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` or `--no-verify` to the `git commit` call, so a future regression that drops the job-level env (e.g. matrix split, reusable-workflow extraction) does not silently reintroduce the commit-msg failure on `entire` absence.
- **`Commit version bump` uses non-conventional prefix `release:`** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:125`. Standard conventional-commits types are `feat/fix/chore/docs/style/refactor/perf/test/build/ci/revert`; `release:` is non-canonical. Harmless under `HUSKY=0` (commit-msg hook skipped), but doubles the failure surface if that env is ever dropped. Trivial rename to `chore(release):` — combine with the `--no-verify` defense-in-depth change above.
- **Git tag pushed before npm publish — OIDC failure orphans the tag on `origin`** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:161-167` (`Create and push tag`) vs `:181` (`Publish to npm via OIDC trusted publishing`). Pre-existing from Story 3.1; Story 3.2 does not introduce but now the `--tag` derivation change advertises a dry-run gate that (correctly) does NOT cover OIDC failures. If OIDC handshake fails after tag-push, `v$VERSION` exists on GitHub without an npm artifact. Belongs with Story 4.1 rollback playbook or a dedicated hardening story (reorder to tag-after-publish, or add a compensating tag-delete step on publish failure).
- **Feature-branch alpha-cut tag commits are orphaned on branch deletion** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:167` (unconditional `git push origin "v..."`) vs `:170` (conditional `Push commit to main` gated on `github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`). On `workflow_dispatch` from a feature branch (Story 3.2 alpha-cut pattern), the `v0.10.1-alpha.0` tag points to a commit that exists only on the feature branch. Post-merge branch deletion may GC that commit depending on GitHub retention; `git describe --tags --abbrev=0` in later runs may fail or pick the wrong previous tag. Interacts with Story 4.1 rollback semantics and with FR / NFR around release-history lineage. Track with Story 3.3 or Story 4.1.
- **Execution-quality note — each failed run required a fresh user-approval click at the env gate.** In a solo-maintainer context (`prevent_self_review: false`), this costs ~5–10s of maintainer time per retry. In a post-Epic-1-Carry-forward-#3 world (second reviewer onboarded, `prevent_self_review: true` flipped), each failed run would require the second reviewer's re-approval — materially increasing iteration cost on workflow defects. NFR1 (15-min CI cycle) re-evaluation for Story 5.1 should account for this. Not a workflow defect; a real-world cost to surface for audit.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-3-merge-release-yaml-to-main-and-retain-legacy-workflows-as-inert-backup (2026-04-21)

- **Non-numeric release version headers bypass section 1→2 transition** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:139`. The awk rule `section==1 && /^## \[[0-9]/` only transitions on `[<digit>`, so hypothetical future tag schemes like `## [v0.11.0]` (with a `v` prefix) or `## [rc-1]` (no leading digit) would be absorbed into the preamble accumulator and the new release would be misplaced. Current SemVer convention always starts with a digit (including pre-release identifiers like `0.10.1-alpha.0`), so this does not fire today. Robustness-only; broaden to `/^## \[/ && !/^## \[Unreleased\]/` when a future story touches this step.
- **Zero-conventional-commit release path — workflow proceeds with empty changelog entry** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:119` (Update CHANGELOG.md step). If there are no conventional commits since the last tag, `conventional-changelog-cli -s` prepends nothing; the preamble-restore guard correctly no-ops, but the workflow continues to tag + publish with no CHANGELOG entry for the new version. Upstream concern (belongs to Update CHANGELOG, not Restore Preamble); candidate for an Epic 4 release-safety hardening pass — detect empty prepend and `exit 1`.
- **Duplicate `# Changelog` headings in malformed input produce duplicated preamble output** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:138`. Rare pre-existing data-corruption scenario (e.g., from a botched merge): the first `# Changelog` line transitions 0→1 correctly; any additional `# Changelog` lines are swept into the preamble accumulator and duplicated in the output. Not introduced by Patch C — any reorder against that input shape is ambiguous. Out of scope until we see one in the wild.
- **No post-restore diff/sanity-check step for CI debuggability** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:149`. Purely diagnostic — a `head -20 CHANGELOG.md` or `git diff CHANGELOG.md | head` after the restore would speed root-causing if the `Assert no bogus issue refs` step ever fails on a mutated file. Not a correctness issue. Bundle with any future release-workflow debugging pass.
- **No end-to-end assertion that `release.yaml` owns the `v*` tag-push path now that `publish.yaml` does not** — `.github/workflows/publish.yaml:4-5` removed `push: tags: - "v*"` (neutralized per Patch A). If `release.yaml` is ever disabled, deleted, or reverted, a `v*` tag push would now silently no-op (no publish, no GitHub Release, no tag-to-artifact linkage). Belongs to Epic 4's RELEASING.md six-scenario rollback playbook — the "release.yaml disabled" scenario needs a runbook entry and monitoring signal. Low probability, high-consequence fallback gap.

## Deferred from: code review of 4-1-author-docs-releasing-md-with-six-scenario-rollback-playbook (2026-04-23)

- **Baseline `jq` filter assumes GET shape matches PUT shape** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+327-332` (Story 4.1). The `### Baseline snapshots` PUT-then-POST recovery applies a `jq '{name, target, enforcement, conditions, rules, bypass_actors}'` filter to a baseline captured from `GET /repos/.../rulesets/<id>` and feeds it to `PUT .../rulesets/<id>`. GitHub's REST API GET vs PUT body shapes for rulesets can differ subtly (e.g., `bypass_actors` reshape, `conditions` nested differently). A round-trip restore drill is the only way to confirm the snippet works end-to-end. Validate against a deliberate ruleset edit + PUT-restore exercise; update the `jq` filter if the PUT 422s.
- **Scenario G detection misses ghost-registered workflows after file deletion** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+240-244` (Story 4.1). GitHub keeps a workflow `state:"active"` for some time after its file is deleted from the default branch. Edge-Hunter live-verified this on the SKF repo: `env-gate-test.yaml` returns `state:"active"` despite the file being absent. If `release.yaml` is deleted via PR, the same lag applies — the AC 9.b detection passes, the next dispatch fails with `Workflow does not exist`, and the responder concludes the playbook check is reliable when it isn't. Augment with a file-presence check on `main` (e.g., `gh api repos/.../contents/.github/workflows/release.yaml`) or `git ls-tree main -- .github/workflows/release.yaml`. Bundle with any future Scenario G hardening pass.
- **Scenario G `gh workflow enable` requires admin permissions** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+252` (Story 4.1). A responder with `triage` or `maintain` role cannot enable workflows; only `admin` can. SKF is single-maintainer today so this gap is dormant. When a second maintainer onboards (Epic 1 Carry-forward #3 — `prevent_self_review: true`), document the ACL requirement and an escalation path or default to the emergency hatch.
- **Scenario A → B boundary blurred by npm CDN cache lag** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+36, +59` (Story 4.1). `npm view <pkg> dist-tags` reads through a CDN with multi-second to multi-minute eventual consistency. "Still shows `<bad>` on `latest`" is not a reliable "no users yet" signal — installs hit different cache nodes. A maintainer running `npm view` 60s after publish can see `<bad>` on `latest` while CI bots in another region have already pulled from a warmer node and committed lockfiles pinning `<bad>`. Add a freshness cross-check (`npm view ... --json | jq '.time | to_entries | last'`) or an explicit "this is a quick visual check, not authoritative" caveat.
- **Scenario C 72h boundary uses npm-clock, not local-clock** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+89` (Story 4.1). The "less than 72 hours since publish" check should be computed from `npm view <pkg>@<bad> time`, not the responder's local `date`. At the boundary, npm's policy engine uses its server timestamp; a guess off by a few minutes 422s the unpublish. Add a one-line "compute from `npm view ... time`" caveat.
- **Scenario C "zero downloads" has 24-48h npm stats lag** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+89` (Story 4.1). The npm downloads API is documented as eventually consistent with up to 24-48h lag; a published version can show "0 downloads" while having been installed by hundreds of CI runs. Treating `npm view` and the implied download count as authoritative in the first 24-48h post-publish is unsafe. Add explicit warning: "the 'zero downloads' criterion is unreliable in the first 24-48h due to npm's stats lag. When in doubt, treat C as Scenario B."
- **Scenario D tag-deletion may be blocked by future tag-protection ruleset** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+121` (Story 4.1). The Default ruleset's `deletion` rule applies to branches; tag protection is a separate ruleset / rule type. The playbook does not state whether tag deletion on `origin` is currently allowed or blocked. If tag-deletion protection is added in any future PR (or if a `tag` rule is added to the existing ruleset), Scenario D's `git push --delete origin v<version>` silently 422s and the recovery dead-ends. Speculative — no rule today; revisit if a `tag` rule is added.
- **Scenario F UI revoke flow assumes 2FA intact** — `docs/RELEASING.md:+188-190` (Story 4.1). The "Visit npmjs.com → Settings → Trusted Publisher → Delete" path is gated on the maintainer's 2FA. The trigger list explicitly includes "npm support notifies of suspected compromise" — a strong signal that account access may itself be compromised. The first-step revoke fails closed in that case; only the later `support@npmjs.com` coordination path is documented as a fallback. Add an "Immediate CLI — account-locked variant" that routes through `support@npmjs.com` *before* attempting the UI flow if 2FA is degraded; reference npm's account-recovery URL.

## Deferred from: code review of 4-2-mark-legacy-release-paths-as-deprecated (2026-04-23)

- **Cross-file anchor `docs/RELEASING.md#rollback-playbook` not covered by CI link validator** — `CONTRIBUTING.md:87` (Story 4.2). `tools/validate-doc-links.js` scans only `docs/`; `tools/validate-file-refs.js` scans only `src/`. Neither catches anchor rot on links from `CONTRIBUTING.md` to `docs/`. If a future RELEASING.md refactor renames `## Rollback Playbook` (or removes it post-Epic-6 cleanup), the CONTRIBUTING link silently 404s on GitHub with no CI signal. Deferred reason: pre-existing validator coverage gap, not introduced by Story 4.2; tooling improvement appropriate post-v1.0.0 alongside the other validator-coverage items captured earlier in this file.
```

Every release commit carries a spurious ~18-line diff on `keywords` alongside the intended 1-line `version` change. No functional impact (prettier `printWidth 140` tolerates both shapes), but reviewers must visually re-verify the reflow is not a regression. Consider `jq -c` with manual JSON shaping, or accept the multi-line normalization as the new canonical form.\n- **`VERSION='${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}'` outer-quote interpolation hazard** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:119` (Story 5.1 H3 fix). GitHub Actions expression templating happens BEFORE bash parses the line; a hypothetical `new_version` containing a literal single quote or newline would terminate the outer string prematurely. SemVer grammar (`[0-9A-Za-z-.]` only in pre-release / build segments) precludes such values, so safe in practice. The accompanying comment (\"`--arg` to avoid shell-quote hazards\") slightly overstates where the safety boundary lives — `--arg` protects the jq filter, not the outer shell. Migrate to `env:` injection of `VERSION` for defense-in-depth.\n- **RC-cut awk preamble-restore classifies `[1.0.0] - TBD` as `section==2`** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:146-163` (existing Story 3.3 awk; interacts with Story 5.1's new CHANGELOG entry). The awk's `section==1 && /^## \\[[0-9]/` transition regex captures the new hand-curated placeholder as \"history\", which is correct behavior under current conventional-changelog-cli semantics (preset prepends at byte 0; output order `[Unreleased]` + `[1.0.0-rc.1]` + `[1.0.0] - TBD` + `[0.10.0]`). Speculative gap: any future minor-version change to conventional-changelog-cli's anchor-emission could silently break the invariant. No assertion in the workflow verifies post-cut final ordering.\n- **CHANGELOG `Assert no bogus issue refs` assertion may false-positive on Story 5.3 reconciliation** — `.github/workflows/release.yaml:171-176` (existing guard; interacts with Story 5.1's new `[1.0.0]` section). The guard greps `^(closes|fixes|resolves) \\[#[^0-9]` across the whole file each release. No current violation — confirmed `grep -iE '(closes|fixes|resolves) \\[#' CHANGELOG.md` returns only numeric issue refs. Preventive flag: if Story 5.3's v1.0.0 final-cut reconciliation introduces `closes [#foo]` into the `[1.0.0]` body (e.g., a \"Breaking changes\" subsection), the workflow aborts mid-release.\n- **Starlight dotted-filename slug-mangling** — `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` (Story 5.1; first dotted `docs/` filename). Astro 5.x + Starlight + github-slugger collapses dots in slug generation: `v1.0.0-launch-audit` → `v100-launch-audit`. Bookmark stability is broken for any file renamed to drop the version. Not a regression; a standing hazard that Story 5.1 introduces via the first dotted filename. Follow-up: document a slug-override convention in CONTRIBUTING.md for version-pinned docs, or prohibit dotted filenames in `docs/`.\n- **Audit Row 7 (TP registration) is inferential, not API-falsifiable** — `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` Row 7 matrix. The npm \"Trusted Publisher\" four-field registration (org / repo / workflow filename / environment) is NOT exposed via public API. The audit's live-proof relies on: (a) `release.yaml` being active, (b) prior alpha tarball carrying SLSA L2 provenance. Between audit-date and next release, a maintainer could silently DELETE the TP registration via the npm UI and no audit path detects it until the next release fails. Acknowledged inferential gap; re-audit cycle at each major release is the compensating control.\n- **Cutting-block verification greps brittle to prettier normalization** — `docs/RELEASING.md:608-609` (Story 5.1 AC 15 verification bullets). Patterns like `grep -c '^## \\[1.0.0-rc.1\\]' CHANGELOG.md` use unescaped dots in the version triplet, which would false-match `1x0x0` (unlikely) and are fragile to future CHANGELOG shapes (trailing date, parenthesized link). Switch to `grep -cE '^## \\[1\\.0\\.0-rc\\.1\\]'` on next edit.\n- **Starlight sidebar IA — `Stability` / `Releasing` appended after `Troubleshooting`** — `website/astro.config.mjs:128-129` (Story 5.1 AC 12). Conventional information architecture ranks API-contract and release-process docs above Troubleshooting in the Reference bucket. Cosmetic; re-order in a future docs-site polish pass.\n- **`## [1.0.0] - TBD` heading lacks compare URL** — `CHANGELOG.md:7` (Story 5.1 AC 13). Historical entries follow `## [X.Y.Z](compare-url) (date)` shape; the placeholder is plain `## [1.0.0] - TBD`. Story 5.3 final-cut reconciliation should add the `compare/v0.10.0...v1.0.0` URL when replacing `TBD` with the publish date — otherwise the heading is visibly inconsistent with siblings.\n- **`### Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1` section length ~52 raw lines vs AC 15's 30–45 target** — `docs/RELEASING.md:559-610` (Story 5.1 AC 15). ## Code review complete.

**2 `decision-needed`, 4 `patch`, 11 `defer`, 19 dismissed as noise.** Findings written to `### Review Findings` in `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/5-1-pre-v1-0-0-readiness-audit.md`; defers also appended under `## Deferred from: code review of 5-1-…` in `deferred-work.md`. Acceptance Auditor reports 22/22 AC compliance — no spec-blocker.

**Decision-needed findings must resolve first.**

---

### Decision 1 of 2 — Audit artifact published to Starlight site with broken links

**Finding:** `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` sits in `docs/`, which is symlinked into `website/src/content/docs/`. That makes it a public Starlight page. Two consequences:

- `./v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` link from `docs/RELEASING.md § Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1` resolves to `./v1.0.0-launch-audit/` in rehype, but the actual emitted slug (github-slugger strips dots) is `v100-launch-audit`. **Link 404s on the published site.**
- 8+ `../_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/*.md` links inside the audit (deferred-work, retros, sibling stories) point outside the content collection. **All render as broken links on the published site.**

**Options:**

1. **Exclude from Starlight** — add a frontmatter flag (`sidebar: { hidden: true }` / `draft: true`) or extend `website/src/content.config.ts` glob filter to skip `v*.md`. Audit remains a GitHub-rendered artifact only (Keep-a-Changelog / release-notes convention). Minimal churn.
2. **Keep public, rewrite links** — convert every `_bmad-output/…` relative link to an absolute `https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/blob/main/...` URL; add a slug override so `./v1.0.0-launch-audit/` resolves. Higher churn, broader-reach doc.
3. **Keep public as-is + accept breakage** — record the link breakage in the audit itself and defer fix to a post-v1.0.0 docs-site polish story.
4. **Defer entire decision** — audit passes clean today; link breakage is a docs-site polish concern, not a release-gate issue. Punt to post-v1.0.0.

**HALT** — pick 1, 2, 3, or 4.

> DEVELOPER

can we move them all in _bmad-output folder? It is a git ignored file. I don't think it is necessary to make it public in the repo. What do you think?

> AGENT

Agree with your instinct — the audit is internal process material, not public contract documentation like STABILITY.md or RELEASING.md. It's a one-shot release-gate artifact full of internal backlog IDs (H1–H10, R1–R8), sign-off placeholders, command transcripts, and cross-references to other `_bmad-output/` files. It doesn't belong alongside the public docs.

One clarification: `_bmad-output/` isn't actually gitignored — it's tracked (sprint-status.yaml, story files, retros, deferred-work.md all live there). But it's NOT symlinked into `website/src/content/docs/`, so anything under `_bmad-output/` is committed-but-not-published. That's exactly the category the audit belongs in.

**Recommended target:** `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` — sibling to story files, retros, and `deferred-work.md` it already references (those links become clean relative neighbors, no `../` hop).

**Scope implications (two tiny follow-ups beyond the move):**

1. `docs/RELEASING.md § Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1` has one backlink (`[v1.0.0-launch-audit.md](./v1.0.0-launch-audit.md)` in Constraints) that needs repointing to an absolute GitHub URL or to `../_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` (relative from `docs/`).
2. Spec AC 1 envelope names `docs/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` as the new file. Strictly, this move deviates from the verbatim envelope — but the envelope's purpose is "audit artifact created", not "at this exact path", and Dev Notes decision (F) explicitly considered the location a judgment call.

Shall I:
- **A)** Move the file, update the RELEASING.md backlink, drop this finding from the decision queue? (also implicitly resolves slug-mangling Defer DF5 for this file)
- **B)** Hold — you want to think about it.

> DEVELOPER

555	The `POST` body needs the same `name`, `target`, `enforcement`, `conditions`, `rules`, and `bypass_actors` fields as the PUT — all captured by the baseline snapshot above. Try `PUT` first; on `404`, `POST` recreates with identical semantics. The ruleset gets a new id on re-creation (IDs are not stable across delete+create cycles); update any hard-coded id references in this document on the next planned edit pass.
556	
557	For the `release` environment, a deletion+restore similarly uses the two-call pattern already documented at `## Release Environment § Restore / re-apply` — feed the environment-level baseline JSON to the `PUT .../environments/release` call, then re-POST each entry from the branch-policies baseline to `.../environments/release/deployment-branch-policies`.
558	
559	### Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1
560	
561	- **Trigger.** Story 5.1 audit ([`v1.0.0-launch-audit.md`](./v1.0.0-launch-audit.md)) signed off and all blockers cleared; Story 5.2 ready to cut the first release-candidate. `package.json` still on a `0.x` line (at audit time: `0.10.0`). Running `release.yaml` with `version_bump: rc` on `0.10.0` would produce `0.10.1-rc.0` — NOT `1.0.0-rc.0` — because `npm version prerelease --preid=rc` advances the patch segment of the current version. The one-time hand-bump below lifts `package.json` onto the `1.0.0` line so the next workflow dispatch advances that line cleanly.
562	- **CLI.**
563	
564	  ```bash
565	  # Hand-bump main onto the 1.0.0 line. Do not tag; release.yaml owns tags.
566	  git checkout main && git pull
567	  npm version 1.0.0-rc.0 --no-git-tag-version
568	  # Leave .claude-plugin/marketplace.json alone — release.yaml's jq-based
569	  # sync step rewrites .plugins[0].version atomically at RC cut time. A
570	  # double-bump here would race against that step.
571	  git add package.json
572	  git commit -m "chore(release): pre-RC bump to 1.0.0-rc.0 (Story 5.1 hand-off)"
573	  # Push via the normal PR path (branch protection blocks direct push to main).
574	  # Merge the PR to main after review.
575	
576	  # Dispatch the canonical release workflow. `version_bump: rc` runs
577	  # `npm version prerelease --preid=rc` on 1.0.0-rc.0 → 1.0.0-rc.1.
578	  gh workflow run release.yaml -f version_bump=rc
579	  ```
580	
581	- **Expected outcome.** `main` tip at `1.0.0-rc.1`; `npm view bmad-module-skill-forge@rc version` returns `1.0.0-rc.1`; a GitHub Release `v1.0.0-rc.1` exists with `prerelease: true` and the auto-generated body; `CHANGELOG.md` now carries an auto-generated `## [1.0.0-rc.1]` entry prepended ABOVE the hand-curated `## [1.0.0] - TBD` placeholder (Story 5.1 staged that placeholder; Story 5.3 reconciles the date at the v1.0.0 final cut); `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`'s `.plugins[0].version` reflects `1.0.0-rc.1`.
582	- **Constraints.**
583	  - Execute ONLY after `v1.0.0-launch-audit.md § Sign-off` is populated with a date.
584	  - The pre-RC bump commit subject MUST be `chore(release): pre-RC bump to 1.0.0-rc.0 (Story 5.1 hand-off)` — use the `chore(release):` prefix (not bare `release:`) or the commit-msg hook rejects it for non-conventional form.
585	  - Do NOT bump `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` in the hand-bump commit. `release.yaml`'s jq-based marketplace sync handles it atomically at RC cut time; a double-bump here would race the workflow.
586	  - The RC cut is the FIRST time the jq-based marketplace.json update path runs in anger. A failure there (jq syntax error, missing `jq` on runner — unlikely on `ubuntu-latest` but treat as a real risk) would halt the cut mid-step, possibly leaving a half-updated `CHANGELOG.md` on `main` with no `marketplace.json` follow-through. Recovery: `git revert` the pre-RC bump PR + retry after fixing the workflow.
587	  - If the workflow dispatch emits `1.0.1-rc.0` instead of `1.0.0-rc.1`, the hand-bump commit never landed on `main` OR `npm version prerelease --preid=rc` behavior diverged from the documented semantics. Abort the RC cut and investigate before retry.
588	- **Verification.**
589

> AGENT

Quick pause — I discovered something that changes the calculus. Let me make sure we're aligned before I execute.

**`.gitignore` line 44 excludes `_bmad-output/` entirely.** Your story file, retros, sprint-status.yaml, deferred-work.md — none of them are tracked. Moving the audit there means **it becomes local-only** (on your machine, not in git, not pushed to GitHub, not reproducible by another maintainer cloning fresh).

Implications:

- **Commit 1 (`540fdda`)'s 7-file envelope drops to 6 files** — the audit file needs a new commit to `git rm` it, and the `_bmad-output/` copy is untracked.
- **`docs/RELEASING.md:561` currently links to `./v1.0.0-launch-audit.md`** — can't link to a gitignored file from a public doc. I'd remove the hyperlink and keep just the text reference ("Story 5.1 audit signed off").
- **Lost:** auditability by future maintainers via git history (e.g., "what was the v1.0.0 gate state?" has no repo answer); disaster-recovery if your machine dies.
- **Gained:** no broken `_bmad-output/…` links; consistent with how every other Story/retro lives.

Three paths:

1. **Local-only (your original proposal)** — move to `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/`, audit becomes gitignored, strip RELEASING.md hyperlink. Cleanest alignment with "it's a process artifact, not a contract doc."
2. **Tracked but not published** — move to a tracked path outside `docs/` (e.g., repo-root `RELEASE-AUDIT-v1.0.0.md` or `.release/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md`), keep it in git so it's shareable + survives machine loss; RELEASING.md backlink becomes an absolute GitHub URL or stays relative.
3. **Keep in docs/, exclude from Starlight** — add a frontmatter flag or content-collection filter; the simplest code change of the three but keeps the audit in a "contract docs" folder it doesn't really belong in.

My read: your instinct pointed at the right problem (it doesn't belong in `docs/`), but **path 2 preserves auditability** without publishing noise. Local-only (path 1) is a real information loss. Path 3 is a band-aid.

Which do you want?

### Decision 2 of 2 — `tools/cli/commands/update.js` has same silent-exit-0 pattern H1 fixed in `install.js`

**Finding:** Dev Notes claimed recon verified "others already exit 1 on failure paths; only install.js has the H1 bug." Edge Hunter re-verified and found `update.js` has the unconditional `process.exit(0)` at function tail regardless of `result.success`. Recon was wrong; H1 fix is incomplete.

**Options:**

1. **Expand scope now** — add `else { console.error(chalk.red('\nUpdate failed.')); process.exit(1); }` to `update.js` on this branch. Violates spec AC 1's seven-file envelope verbatim, but the envelope's purpose is "surgical audit", not "strictly 7 files" — and the whole point of H1 was closing silent-exit-0 bugs in the CLI. Fix is two lines. Audit artifact's H1 disposition needs a line-item update to mention both files.
2. **Also audit `status.js` and `uninstall.js` while we're here** — full CLI sweep. Either confirms the recon (only install + update have it) or surfaces more. Two additional greps, possibly one more 2-line patch per file.
3. **Defer to a follow-up story** — file it under `deferred-work.md` as an H1 completeness gap. RC cycle (Story 5.2–5.4) is not blocked by this because `update` isn't on the RC release critical path.

My read: **(2)** is the right call — the recon was demonstrably wrong, so trusting the remaining claim ("status/uninstall already exit 1") without re-verifying is risky. The full sweep is cheap. If status/uninstall are clean, apply just the `update.js` patch. If not, apply all needed.

Which do you want — 1, 2, or 3?

1	/**
2	 * SKF Quick Update Command
3	 * Replaces SKF files and reinstalls agent skill without re-prompting.
4	 * Preserves config.yaml and sidecar state.
5	 */
6	
7	const chalk = require('chalk');
8	const path = require('node:path');
9	const fs = require('fs-extra');
10	const yaml = require('js-yaml');
11	const { Installer } = require('../lib/installer');
12	const { UI } = require('../lib/ui');
13	
14	const SKF_FOLDER = '_bmad/skf';
15	
16	module.exports = {
17	  command: 'update',
18	  description: 'Update SKF files and reinstall agent skill (preserves config and sidecar)',
19	  options: [],
20	  action: async () => {
21	    try {
22	      const projectDir = process.cwd();
23	      const skfDir = path.join(projectDir, SKF_FOLDER);
24	
25	      if (!(await fs.pathExists(skfDir))) {
26	        console.log(chalk.yellow('\n  SKF is not installed in this directory.'));
27	        console.log(chalk.dim('  Run: npx bmad-module-skill-forge install\n'));
28	        process.exit(0);
29	        return;
30	      }
31	
32	      console.log('');
33	      console.log(chalk.hex('#F59E0B').bold('  Skill Forge — Quick Update'));
34	      console.log(chalk.dim('  Replacing SKF files, preserving config and sidecar.\n'));
35	
36	      const installer = new Installer();
37	      const result = await installer.install({
38	        projectDir,
39	        skfFolder: SKF_FOLDER,
40	        _action: 'update',
41	      });
42	
43	      if (result && result.success) {
44	        // Read config to get IDEs for post-update notes
45	        let ides = [];
46	        try {
47	          const configContent = await fs.readFile(path.join(skfDir, 'config.yaml'), 'utf8');
48	          const config = yaml.load(configContent);
49	          ides = config?.ides || [];
50	        } catch {
51	          /* use empty */
52	        }
53	        const ui = new UI();
54	        ui.displaySuccess(SKF_FOLDER, ides, 'update');
55	      }
56	
57	      process.exit(0);
58	    } catch (error) {
59	      console.error(chalk.red('\nUpdate failed:'), error.message);
60	      process.exit(1);
61	    }
62	  },
63	};
64
